Definition 6.36
.

For
E/F,\alpha_{0}
and
\xi
as above, write
\chi_{m}
for any choice of character of
F^{\times}
satisfying

(1)

\chi_{m}(2)=1
, and

(2)

when
d=0
or
2
,
\chi_{m}(1+x)=\psi(z\frac{A}{2}x)
for all
x
with
v(x)\geq\lceil\frac{-v(zA/2)}{2}\rceil
.

When
d=3
set
\chi_{m}=1
.

Proposition 6.37
.

Suppose that
\sigma
is a trivial central character supercuspidal representation that corresponds by the LLC to
\Ind_{E}^{F}\xi
with
c(\xi)\geq 2
. Suppose
\alpha_{0}
is a normalized minimal element for
E/F
with minimal polynomial as in Lemma
6.31
. Then,
\sigma\times\chi_{m}^{-1}
is twist-minimal and moreover we have

\min_{\chi}c(\sigma\times\chi)=\begin{cases}c(\sigma)-2=2j&\text{ if }d=0,\\
c(\sigma)-1=2j+1&\text{ if }d=2,\\
c(\sigma)=2j+1&\text{ if }d=3.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
\rho=\Ind_{E}^{F}\xi
be the Galois representation corresponding to
\sigma
by the LLC.
We have
\rho\otimes\chi=\Ind_{E}^{F}\xi\chi_{E}
, where
\chi_{E}=\chi\circ\Nm
. By (
6.8
), the formula
c(\xi)=-v_{E}(\alpha_{\xi})+c(\psi_{E})
of Lemma
6.1
, the Artin conductor
c(\rho\otimes\chi)
is minimized when the valuation of

\alpha_{\xi\chi_{E}}=\alpha_{\xi}+\alpha_{\chi}=z\frac{A}{2}+z\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{\chi}

is maximized. Since
\alpha_{\chi}\in\mathbb{Q}_{2}
, we have by Lemma
6.3

(6.34)

v_{E}(\alpha_{\xi\chi_{E}})=\min(v_{E}(z\frac{A}{2}+\alpha_{\chi}),v_{E}(z\alpha_{0})),

which can be maximized by taking
\alpha_{\chi}=-z\frac{A}{2}
, matching Definition
6.36
of
\chi_{m}^{-1}
. This proves the first assertion of the proposition.

Computing the conductor of
c(\sigma\times\chi_{m}^{-1})
in cases using (
6.8
),
c(\xi)=-v_{E}(\alpha_{\xi})+c(\psi_{E})
, (
6.34
), Lemma
6.34
, and (
6.2
), we conclude the formula for
\min_{\chi}c(\sigma\times\chi)
in the second assertion.
∎

Given
\sigma,E/F,\alpha_{0}
as in Proposition
6.37
, let
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\times\chi_{m}^{-1}
denote the underlying twist-minimal representation. Let
\rho^{\prime}=\Ind_{E}^{F}\xi^{\prime}
be the corresponding Weil group representation under the LLC. We have
\alpha_{\xi^{\prime}}=z\alpha_{0}
for

as in Lemma
6.34
and Remark

.
If

\varphi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}

), and if

\varphi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}
be defined by
[

, Def. 3.12]
.

If

is ramified and

is sufficiently large in the sense of Lemma
6.15
, then we explained in Section

the construction of a character
\theta^{\prime}
of

that leads to
\sigma^{\prime}
by compact induction. Precisely, if

(6.35)

c(\sigma^{\prime})\geq\begin{cases}7&\text{ if }d=2,\\
11&\text{ if }d=3,\end{cases}

then Corollary
6.17
applies, so that in particular
c(\theta^{\prime})=c(\xi^{\prime})
.

If
E/F
is unramified, then let
\theta^{\prime}
be the character of
E^{\times}
corresponding to
\sigma^{\prime}
across the compact induction bijection (
6.1.3
). Recall that
\theta^{\prime}=\xi^{\prime}\Delta_{\xi^{\prime}}
with
\Delta_{\xi^{\prime}}
unramified in this case
[

, §34.4]
.

and

.

We take

, where, following section
6.1.3
, the representation

is constructed from the above
\theta^{\prime}
on

by (

) with respect to

and

\langle,\rangle

and

\Phi_{\varphi}|_{ZB^{1}}&\text{ if }E/F\text{ is unramified}\end{cases}\text{ and }V=\begin{cases}\vol(Z\backslash H)&\text{ if }E/F\text{ is ramified }\\
\vol(Z\backslash ZB^{1})&\text{ if }E/F\text{ is unramified}.\end{cases}

Lemma 6.38
.

We have that

(6.37)

V=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(1-p^{-2})p^{c_{0}+1}}&\text{ if }d=0\text{ or }2,\\
\frac{1}{(1-p^{-2})p^{c_{0}+2}}&\text{ if }d=3.\end{cases}

Proof.

First consider the case that
d=0
. If
c_{0}
is even we have that
ZB^{1}=ZU_{E}(1)U_{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}^{c_{0}/2}
, and if
c_{0}
is odd we have that
ZB^{1}
is a proper intermediate subgroup between
ZH^{1}=ZU_{E}(1)U_{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}^{\lceil c_{0}/2\rceil}
and
ZJ^{1}=ZU_{E}(1)U_{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}^{\lfloor c_{0}/2\rfloor}
. Lemma
6.33
computes the volumes of these groups, giving (
6.37
) in both
d=0
cases. Next consider the case that
E/F
is ramified, in which we have
j=c_{0}
if
d=2
and
j=c_{0}+1
when
d=3
by Remark
6.35
. The formula for
V
then follows directly from Lemma
6.33
. ∎

Recall the neighborhood
\theta[n]
of characters around
\theta
from (
6.10
).

Proposition 6.39
.

Suppose
\sigma,E/F,\alpha_{0},\ell,j
are as above and satisfy
2-e\leq\ell\leq\min(j,c(\theta^{\prime})-1)
. The operator
\pi(\overline{\widetilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})
vanishes unless there exists
\theta_{1}\in\theta[\ell]

such that
\pi\simeq\cInd_{J}^{G}\Lambda_{1}
, and in that case
V^{-1}\pi(\overline{\widetilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})
is a projection onto the line of the minimal vector in
\pi
.

Proof.

First assume that
E/F
is ramified.
We will show that if
\pi(\overline{\widetilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})
is non-trivial, then
\pi\simeq\cInd_{J}^{G}\widetilde{\theta}_{1}
for some
\theta_{1}\in\theta[\ell]
.

Taking
\sigma=\cInd_{J}^{G}\tilde{\theta}
, we have
\sigma(u)\varphi=\tilde{\theta}(u)\varphi
for
u\in J
. Let
v\in\pi
be any vector such that
\pi(\overline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})v\neq 0
. Then, define the non-zero linear map
\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}|_{H}\to\pi\times\chi_{m}^{-1}|_{H}
by
z\mapsto z\pi(\overline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})v.
We check that it is
H
-equivariant: Any
g\in H
acts by

\displaystyle(\pi\times\chi_{m}^{-1})(g)\left[z\pi(\overline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})v\right]

\displaystyle=z\int_{H}\overline{\langle\sigma(h)\varphi,\varphi\rangle}(\pi\times\chi_{m}^{-1})(g)\pi(h)v\,dh

\displaystyle=\chi_{m}^{-1}(\det g)z\int_{H}\overline{\langle\sigma(h)\varphi,\varphi\rangle}\pi(gh)v\,dh

\displaystyle=\chi_{m}^{-1}(\det g)z\int_{H}\overline{\langle\sigma(h)\varphi,\sigma(g)\varphi\rangle}\pi(h)v\,dh

(6.38)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}(g)z\pi(\overline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})v.

Therefore, we have

(6.39)

0\neq\text{Hom}_{H}(\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}|_{H},\pi\times\chi_{m}^{-1}|_{H})=\text{Hom}_{G}(\cInd_{H}^{G}(\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}|_{H}),\pi\times\chi_{m}^{-1}).

Next, we claim that

(6.40)

\Ind_{H}^{J}(\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}|_{H})=\bigoplus\limits_{\theta_{1}^{\prime}\in\theta^{\prime}[l]}\tilde{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}.

By Postnikov,
\alpha_{\theta^{\prime}}-\alpha_{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}\equiv 0\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{E}^{c(\psi_{E})-\ell}}
. Then, for any
x\in U_{\mathfrak{A}_{e}}^{j}
, we have that
\psi(\Tr((\alpha_{\theta^{\prime}}-\alpha_{\theta_{1}^{\prime}})x))=1
by the hypothesis that
j\geq\ell
. Since
\theta_{1}\in\theta[\ell]
, we have
c(\theta_{1}\theta^{-1})\leq\ell
, so that
\tilde{\theta}_{1}^{\prime}=\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}
on
H
. Then,

\mathbb{C}=\text{Hom}_{H}(\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}|_{H},\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}_{1}|_{H})=\text{Hom}_{J}(\Ind_{H}^{J}\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}|_{H},\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}_{1}).

Therefore, each
\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}_{1}
is a sub-representation of
\Ind_{H}^{J}\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}|_{H}
, and occurs only once as a sub-representation of it. Moreover,
\theta^{\prime}[\ell]
is simply a translate of
\widehat{J/H}
, so the dimension of both sides of (
6.40
) are equal, and therefore the sum of these 1-dimensional sub-representations exhausts
\Ind_{H}^{J}\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}
.

Since
\cInd
is additive and transitive, it follows from (
6.40
) that

(6.41)

\cInd_{H}^{G}(\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}|_{H})=\bigoplus\limits_{\theta_{1}^{\prime}\in\theta^{\prime}[l]}\cInd_{J}^{G}\tilde{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}.

Next we claim that if
\theta^{\prime}
is minimal and
c(\theta^{\prime})\geq\ell+1
,
\ell\geq 0
, then all
\theta_{1}^{\prime}\in\theta^{\prime}[\ell]
are minimal. Indeed, since
c(\theta^{\prime}_{1}\theta^{\prime-1})\leq\ell
, we have that
\theta^{\prime}_{1}|_{U(\ell)}=\theta^{\prime}|_{U(\ell)}
. Since
c(\theta^{\prime})\geq\ell+1
, we have
\theta^{\prime}_{1}|_{U(c(\theta^{\prime})-1)}=\theta^{\prime}|_{U(c(\theta^{\prime})-1)}
, so that
c(\theta^{\prime})=c(\theta^{\prime}_{1})
and also
\chi_{E}\theta^{\prime}_{1}|_{U(c(\theta^{\prime})-1)}=\chi_{E}\theta^{\prime}|_{U(c(\theta^{\prime})-1)}
for any
\chi
.
Since
\theta^{\prime}
is minimal,
\chi_{E}\theta^{\prime}
is non-trivial on
U(c(\theta^{\prime})-1)
. Thus,
\chi_{E}\theta^{\prime}_{1}
is non-trivial on
U(c(\theta^{\prime})-1)=U(c(\theta^{\prime}_{1})-1)
. That is to say,
c(\chi_{E}\theta^{\prime}_{1})\geq c(\theta^{\prime}_{1})
for all
\chi
.

The representations
\cInd_{J}^{G}\tilde{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}
are irreducible and supercuspidal by
[
BH06
, 15.3 Thm.]
provided we check hypotheses as follows. Since
\theta_{1}^{\prime}
is minimal, the element
\alpha_{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}\in E^{\times}
is minimal by Lemma
6.5
and so by 13.5 Prop. of loc. cit. we have that there exists a chain order
\mathfrak{A}
such that
(\mathfrak{A},-v_{E}(\alpha_{\theta^{\prime}_{1}}),\alpha_{\theta^{\prime}_{1}})
is a simple stratum. Since
c(\theta_{1}^{\prime})=c(\theta^{\prime})
, we have that
n:=-v_{E}(\theta_{1}^{\prime})=-v_{E}(\theta^{\prime})
so that
J_{\alpha}
with
\alpha=\alpha_{\theta^{\prime}_{1}}
as defined in loc. cit. (15.3.1) matches
J
as defined in (
6.17
) with respect to the character
\theta^{\prime}
. Moreover,
\widetilde{\theta}_{1}^{\prime}
is a
1
-dimensional representation of
J
that by definition contains the character
\psi_{\alpha}
of
U_{\mathfrak{A}_{e}}^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor+1}
.

Since the
\cInd_{J}^{G}\tilde{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}
on the right of (
6.41
) are irreducible, we conclude from (
6.39
) the claim in the second sentence of the proof.

Choose an orthonormal basis
\mathcal{B}_{\pi}
for
\pi
that contains the minimal vector, say,
\phi
. Then, by definition
H
acts through
\pi
on
\phi
by
\pi(h)\phi=\theta_{1}(h)\phi
for
h\in H
. Now, we also have
\overline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi}(h)=\overline{\theta}(h)
for
h\in H
by Lemma
6.18
. So, for any
v\in\mathcal{B}_{\pi}
we have

\langle\pi(\overline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})v,\phi\rangle=\langle v,\int_{H}\overline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi}(h^{-1})\pi(h)\phi dh\rangle=\vol(Z\backslash H)\langle v,\phi\rangle,

since
\theta=\theta_{1}
on
U(l)
. So, for
v\in\mathcal{B}_{\pi}
, we have
\pi(\overline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})v=0
unless
\varphi=\phi
. Thus,
\frac{1}{\vol(Z\backslash H)}\pi(\overline{\tilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})
is a projection onto the line of the minimal vector in
\pi
.

Now suppose that
E/F
is unramified. If
\pi(\overline{\widetilde{\Phi}}_{\varphi})
is non-trivial then the same calculation as (
6.3
) with
H
replaced by
ZB^{1}
shows that there exists a vector
v\in\pi
such that
(\pi\times\chi_{m}^{-1})(b)v=\widetilde{\theta}^{\prime}(b)v
for all
b\in ZB^{1}
. Then, the assertions of the proposition follow from
[
Hu24
, Props. 3.14 and 3.21]
applied to
\pi\times\chi_{m}^{-1}
.
∎

For a moment let us consider the more general situation that
\sigma
is a smooth irreducible representation of
G(F)
endowed with a unitary pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\sigma}
. Let
V
be the space of functions
f
on
G(F)
satisfying
f(ng)=\psi(n)f(g)
for all
n\in N(F)
and
g\in G(F)
. For any
g_{0}\in G(F)
and
v^{\prime}\in\sigma
, let
W:\sigma\to V
be defined by

(6.42)

W:v\mapsto W_{v}(g)=\int_{N(F)}\langle\sigma(g_{0}ng)v,v^{\prime}\rangle_{\sigma}\psi(-n)\,dn.

One can directly check that

W_{v}(ng)=\psi(n)W_{v}(g)\quad\text{ and }\quad W_{\sigma(h)v}(g)=W_{v}({gh}).

Thus, if
g_{0}
and
v^{\prime}
are be chosen so that the map
W
is non-zero, then it follows that
W
is an isomorphism onto the Whittaker model of
\sigma
.

Let us return now to the situation at hand introduced just before Proposition
6.39
. As in
[
Hu24
, §3.2.2]
we can compute the Whittaker function
W_{\varphi^{\prime}}
of the minimal vector
\varphi^{\prime}
in the twist-minimal representation
\sigma^{\prime}
using (
6.42
).

Lemma 6.40
.

Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be a minimal vector in a twist-minimal dihedral supercuspidal representation
\sigma^{\prime}
of sufficiently large conductor in the sense (
6.35
).
Its Whittaker function along the diagonal
W_{\varphi^{\prime}}(a(x))
is, up to a scalar, equal to
1_{-zBU_{F}(\lceil j/2\rceil)}
, where
z
and
B
are as in Lemma
6.34
with respect to an additive character
\psi
with
c(\psi)=0
, and
j
is as in Remark
6.35
.

Proof.

The case that
E/F
is unramified is given by
[
Hu24
, Lem. 3.15]
, whose proof goes through with the extra assumption that
\sigma^{\prime}
is twist-minimal to permit the use of
[
BH06
, 15.3 Thm.]
in the final step.

We therefore assume that
E/F
is ramified for the rest of the proof.
We compute the integral in (
6.42
) along
A(F)
using Lemma
6.18
. To do so, we need to explicate the
J,\theta^{\prime}
used to construct
\sigma^{\prime}
by compact induction.

Recall the character
\theta^{\prime}
of
E^{\times}
defined just above Corollary
6.17
that gives rise to
\sigma^{\prime}
by compact induction. Since
F=\mathbb{Q}_{2}
, the hypothesized lower bound on
c(\sigma^{\prime})
implies the condition of Lemma
6.15
is satisfied, so that by Corollary
6.17
we have

\alpha_{\theta^{\prime}}\equiv\alpha_{\xi^{\prime}}\pmod{\mathfrak{p}_{E}^{-j+c(\psi_{E})}}
.

Now let
z\in F^{\times}
be as in Lemma
6.34
and Remark
6.35
, so that
\alpha_{\xi^{\prime}}=z\alpha_{0}
.
Therefore, choosing the embedding
E^{\times}\hookrightarrow G(F)
of (
6.1.1
) in terms of
\alpha_{0}
, we have that
\alpha_{\theta^{\prime}}
is given in matrix form by

\alpha_{\theta^{\prime}}=z\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
-B&-A\end{pmatrix}\pmod{\mathfrak{P}_{2}^{-j+c(\psi_{E})}}.

We choose
v^{\prime}
and
g_{0}
in (
6.42
) to be given by
v^{\prime}=\varphi^{\prime}
and
g_{0}=a(-1/zB)
, and
c(\psi)=0
. By Lemma
6.32
and since

U_{\mathfrak{A}_{2}}^{j}=1+\mathfrak{P}_{2}^{j}=1+\begin{pmatrix}p^{\lceil j/2\rceil}\mathcal{O}&p^{\lfloor j/2\rfloor}\mathcal{O}\\
p^{\lfloor j/2\rfloor+1}\mathcal{O}&p^{\lceil j/2\rceil}\mathcal{O}\end{pmatrix}

we have

g_{0}na(x)\in J\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad\begin{cases}x\in-zBU_{F}(\lceil j/2\rceil)\text{ and }\\
v(n)\geq-\lceil j/2\rceil.\end{cases}

Thus by Lemma
6.18
we have

(6.43)

\displaystyle W_{\varphi^{\prime}}(a(x))

\displaystyle=\langle\varphi^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime}\rangle\int\limits_{v(n)\geq-\lceil j/2\rceil}\psi\circ\Tr\left(z\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-B&-A\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{x}{zB}-1&-\frac{1}{zB}n\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\right)\psi(-n)dn

\displaystyle=\langle\varphi^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime}\rangle\int\limits_{v(n)\geq-\lceil j/2\rceil}dn.

∎

By translating the minimal vector
\varphi^{\prime}
back to the minimal vector
\varphi\in\cInd_{J}^{G}\Lambda\simeq\sigma
, we have that the
L^{2}
-normalized (
6.25
) Whittaker function of
\varphi
satisfies

(6.44)

W_{\varphi}(a(x))=\vol(U_{F}(\lceil j/2\rceil))^{-1/2}\chi_{m}(x)1_{-zBU_{F}(\lceil j/2\rceil)}(x).

Next we express new vectors as a sum of translates of the minimal vector. We shall only need formulas up to constants at this point, as these will be nailed down later in Proposition
6.44
after giving an alternate description for (a sum of conjugates of) the test function
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varphi}
defined in (
6.36
) (cf. Proposition
6.39
). The notation
\propto
denotes equality up to a constant.

Lemma 6.41
.

Let
\varphi\in\sigma
be the
\chi_{m}
-translate of the minimal vector
\varphi^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{0}\in\sigma
be a newvector. Then

(1)

when
d=0
or
2
,

(6.45)

\varphi_{0}\propto\sum\limits_{b\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}/U(j+1)}\chi_{m}(b)\sigma\Big(\begin{pmatrix}1&p^{-j-1}b\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\Big)\sum\limits_{a\in(\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{p}^{\lceil j/2\rceil})^{\times}}\chi_{m}^{-1}(a)\sigma\Big(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\Big)\varphi,

(2)

when
d=3

(6.46)

\varphi_{0}\propto\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}/U(\lceil j/2\rceil)}\sigma\Big(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\Big)\varphi.

Proof.

We compute in the Kirillov model
\mathcal{K}(\sigma,\psi)
using (
6.44
), following
[
Hu24
, §3.2.2, 3.3.2]
. We focus on the
d=0,2
case as the
d=3
case is strictly simpler.

Consider the interior sum. Since
\chi_{m}(p)=1
by definition, we have by (
6.44
) that

\sum\limits_{a\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}/U(\lceil j/2\rceil)}\chi_{m}^{-1}(a)\sigma\Big(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\Big)W_{\varphi}(a(x))\propto\chi_{m}(x)1_{\mathcal{O}^{\times}}(x)\in\mathcal{K}(\sigma,\psi).

By the defining property of the Whittaker model, the function on the right hand side of (
6.45
) in the Kirillov model is proportional to

\sum\limits_{b\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}/U(j+1)}\chi_{m}(b)\psi\left(\frac{bx}{p^{j+1}}\right)\chi_{m}(x)1_{\mathcal{O}^{\times}}(x).

The character
\chi_{m}
has conductor
j+1
in either case
d=0
or
d=2
(see Definition
6.36
), so that the Gauss sum above satisfies

\sum\limits_{b\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}/U(j+1)}\chi_{m}(b)\psi\left(\frac{bx}{p^{j+1}}\right)\propto\chi_{m}(x)^{-1}

for
x\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
(cf.
[
Hu24
, Lem. 3.25]
).
∎

Now we give a preliminary definition of the newform projector in the
p=2
case. For
\varphi\in\sigma
the minimal vector and
\ell\geq 2-e
recall the restricted matrix coefficient
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varphi}
and volume
V
from (
6.36
). Let
c
be the constant of proportionality in Lemma
6.41
, so that
c^{-1}\varphi_{0}
is equal to the expression on the right hand sides of either (
6.45
) and (
6.46
) as appropriate.

Definition 6.42
.

Set
f\in\mathcal{H}_{2}
to be the function satisfying

(1)

when
d=0
or
2

\overline{f(g)}=c^{2}V^{-1}\sum\limits_{b,b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}/U(j+1)}\sum\limits_{a,a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}/U(\lceil j/2\rceil)}\chi_{m}\left(\frac{ba^{\prime}}{ab^{\prime}}\right)\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varphi}\left(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a^{\prime}&p^{-j-1}b^{\prime}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}^{-1}g\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a&p^{-j-1}b\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\right),

(2)

when
d=3

\overline{f(g)}=c^{2}V^{-1}\sum\limits_{a,a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}/U(\lceil j/2\rceil)}\tilde{\Phi}_{\varphi}\left(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a^{\prime}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}^{-1}g\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\right).

Corollary 6.43

(of Proposition
6.39
and Lemma
6.41
)
.

For
\sigma
a trivial central character supercuspial representation of
G(F)
with

c(\sigma)\geq\begin{cases}5&\text{ if }d=0\\
8&\text{ if }d=2,\\
11&\text{ if }d=3\end{cases}

and
\ell
as in Proposition
6.39
,

(1)

the
f\in\mathcal{H}_{2}
constructed from these as in Definition
6.42
is a newform projector in the sense of Definition
1.5
, and

(2)

the operator
\pi(f)
is 0 unless there exists
\theta_{1}\in\theta[\ell]
such that
\pi\simeq\cInd_{J}^{G}\Lambda_{1}
.

Here, recall that if
E/F
is ramified, we have
\Lambda_{1}=\widetilde{\theta}_{1}
where
\widetilde{\theta}
is the extension of
\theta
from
E^{\times}
to
J
in (
6.18
). If
E/F
is unramified, then
\Lambda_{1}
is constructed from
\theta_{1}
as in
[
Hu24
, §3.2.1]
.

We have shown that
f
satisfies the spectral assumption for an appropriate choice of scalar. Now, we give an alternate description of
f
from which the geometric assumption is obvious, and which moreover allows us to pin down the choice of scalar.

Proposition 6.44
.

Suppose
F
and
c(\sigma)
are as in Corollary
6.43
, and choose
\ell=1
.

(1)

If
d=0
,

f=V^{-1}\overline{\Phi}_{\varphi_{0}}|_{ZK_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}=\frac{1}{\|\Phi_{\varphi_{0}}|_{ZK_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}\|_{2}^{2}}\overline{\Phi}_{\varphi_{0}}|_{ZK_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}.

(2)

If
d=2
, we have

f=V^{-1}\overline{\Phi}_{\varphi_{0}}|_{ZK_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}=\frac{1}{\|\Phi_{\varphi_{0}}|_{ZK_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}\|_{2}^{2}}\overline{\Phi}_{\varphi_{0}}|_{ZK_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}.

(3)

If
d=3
, we have

f=V^{-1}\overline{\Phi}_{\varphi_{0}}|_{ZK_{0}(j+1,-j)}=\frac{1}{\|\Phi_{\varphi_{0}}|_{K_{0}(j+1,-j)}\|_{2}^{2}}\overline{\Phi}_{\varphi_{0}}|_{K_{0}(j+1,-j)}.

Proof.

We assume that
d=2
, as the
d=0,3
cases are simpler.
By Lemma
6.41
,

\Phi_{\varphi_{0}}(g)=c^{2}\sum\limits_{b,b^{\prime},a,a^{\prime}}\chi\left(\frac{ba^{\prime}}{ab^{\prime}}\right){\Phi}_{\varphi}\left(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a^{\prime}&p^{-j-1}b^{\prime}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}^{-1}g\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a&p^{-j-1}b\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\right).

We claim that for any
h\in H=ZU_{E}(\ell)U_{\mathfrak{A}_{e}}^{j}

(6.47)

\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a^{\prime}&p^{-j-1}b^{\prime}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}h\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a&p^{-j-1}b\\
&1\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\in ZK_{0}(j+1,-j-1),

and for any
h\in E^{\times}U_{\mathfrak{A}_{e}}^{j}\smallsetminus H
,

(6.48)

\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a^{\prime}&p^{-j-1}b^{\prime}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}h\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a&p^{-j-1}b\\
&1\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\not\in ZK_{0}(j+1,-j-1).

When
h\in H
there exists
s\in F^{\times}
,
y,z\in F
such that

(6.49)

h=s(y+z\alpha_{0})(1+x)=s\begin{pmatrix}y&z\\
-zB&y-Az\end{pmatrix}(1+x)

with
1+x\in U_{\mathfrak{A}_{e}}^{j}
,
v(y-1)\geq 1
and
v(z)\geq 0
. Then, using Lemma
6.31
we can check directly by computing the valuation of each entry that (
6.47
) is true.

Now suppose that
h\in E^{\times}U_{\mathfrak{A}_{e}}^{j}\smallsetminus ZU_{E}(1)U_{\mathfrak{A}_{e}}^{j}
. If we can write
h
as
h=s(y+z\alpha_{0})(1+x)
with
y\neq 0
, then
h=sy(1+\frac{z}{y}\alpha_{0})(1+x)
, so that since
h\not\in H
we must have
v_{E}(z\alpha_{0}/y)\leq 0
, i.e.
v(z)+1\leq v(y)
. Now we look at the valuation of the determinant of
h
, which is
v(y^{2}-Ayz+z^{2}B)+2v(s)
, but we have (by Lemma
6.31
) that

v(z^{2}B)=2v(z)+1\leq v(zy)<v(Azy)\leq v(y^{2}).

Thus,
v(\det(h))=v(z^{2}B)+2v(s)
. If on the other hand
y=0
, then we have directly that
v(\det(h))=v(z^{2}B)+2v(s)
. In either case,
v(\det(h))
is odd, which proves (
6.48
).

We thus have that

\Phi_{\varphi_{0}}|_{K_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}=c^{2}\sum\limits_{b,b^{\prime},a,a^{\prime}}\chi\left(\frac{ba^{\prime}}{ab^{\prime}}\right)\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varphi}\left(\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a^{\prime}&p^{-j-1}b^{\prime}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}^{-1}g\begin{pmatrix}p^{-j}a&p^{-j-1}b\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\right).

This establishes the first equality of the Proposition.

For the second equality,
f=V^{-1}\overline{\Phi}_{\varphi_{0}}|_{K_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}
is a newform projector, so by Lemma
4.7
we have

\|\Phi_{\varphi_{0}}|_{K_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}\|_{2}^{2}=V\overline{\Phi}_{\varphi_{0}}|_{K_{0}(j+1,-j-1)}(1)=V\overline{\Phi}_{\varphi_{0}}(1)=V.

∎

Proof of Theorem
6.29
.

Combine Corollary
6.43
(1) and Proposition
6.44
to see that relevant test functions are newform projectors. Combine Remark
6.10
and Table
1
with Corollary
6.43
(2) for the assertions on the size of the support of
\pi(f)
.
∎

6.4.
Local generalized Kloosterman sums for supercuspidal representations

Let
\sigma
be a trivial central character dihedral supercuspidal representation of
\GL_{2}(F)
and
(E/F,\xi)
be a pair such that
\sigma
correspond to
\Ind_{E}^{F}\xi
by the LLC.
Let
H_{p}(m,n;c)
be the local Kloosterman sum (see (
3.14
)) associated with the test function
f=f_{\xi}
defined in either Theorem
6.20
(
p
odd) or Theorem
6.29
(
p
even). If
p=2
assume further that
c(\sigma)
is sufficiently large in the sense of Theorem
6.29
.

Theorem 6.45
.

If
k<\lceil c(\sigma)/2\rceil
, then
H_{p}(m,n;p^{k})
vanishes identically. If
k\geq\lceil c(\sigma)/2\rceil
, then
H_{p}(m,n;p^{k})
is given by

(6.50)

H_{p}(m,n;p^{k})=\overline{\gamma}(1-p^{-1})^{-1}f_{\xi}(1)p^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p^{k}\mathcal{O}_{E})^{\times}\\
\Nm(u)\equiv mn\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\xi(u)\psi\left(-\frac{\Tr(u)}{p^{k}}\right),

where
\gamma\in S^{1}
depends only on the isomorphism class of
E
and the choice of additive character
\psi
. In particular,
H_{p}(m,n;p^{k})=0
if
(mn,p)\neq 1
.

Remark 6.46
.

More precisely,
\gamma=\lambda(E,\psi)
is the Langlands constant as in
[
JL70
, Lem. 1.2(iv)]
. For an explicit description of
\gamma
, see
[
BH06
, §34.3]
.
Note that explicit formulas for
f_{\xi}(1)
were given in (
6.24
) for
p
odd and in (
6.31
) for
p=2
.

Remark 6.47
.

By Theorem
6.29
, when
p=2
and
E/F
is the unramified extension the sum on the right hand side of (
6.50
) may be restricted to
U_{E}(1)/U_{E}(k)
without changing the validity of the equation. This assertion can also be (sanity) checked by decomposing
u=u_{0}+du
with
v_{E}(du)\geq 1
, using Lemma
6.1
, and noting that
U_{\mathbb{Q}_{2}}(0)=U_{\mathbb{Q}_{2}}(1)
.

Proof.

Recalling the definition of
H_{p}(m,n;c)
from (
3.14
) we choose the test function
f=f_{p}
in the
p
odd case from Theorem
6.20
and in the
p=2
case from Theorem
6.29
. Such an
f
has support contained in
a(y)^{-1}ZKa(y)
with

(6.51)

y=\begin{cases}p^{c_{0}}&\text{ if }d=0,\\
p^{c_{0}+1}&\text{ if }d=1\text{ or }2,\\
p^{c_{0}+2}&\text{ if }d=3.\end{cases}

We can unify these (see (
6.8
)) as

v_{p}(y)=\lceil c(\sigma)/2\rceil.

If
k<\lceil c(\sigma)/2\rceil
, then
H_{p}(m,n,p^{k})=0
by Lemma
3.5
. This proves the first assertion of the Theorem.
We now assume for the rest of the proof that
k\geq v_{p}(y)
, equivalently
2k\geq c(\sigma)
.

Lemma 6.48
.

For the above choice of
f
, when
2k\geq c(\sigma)
we have

(6.52)

H_{p}(m,n;p^{k})=f_{\xi}(1)\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(t_{2})\geq-k\\
v(t_{1})\geq-k\end{subarray}}\overline{\Phi}\left(n(t_{1})^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})\right)\psi(-mt_{1}+nt_{2})\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}.

If moreover
2k>c(\sigma)
, then one or both conditions
v(t_{i})\geq-k
on the integration may be replaced by
v(t_{i})=-k
.

Proof.

By looking at the determinant (cf. (
3.8
)) we have that
v(t_{1})
and
v(t_{2})\geq-k
whenever
n(t_{1})^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})
is in a diagonal conjugate of
ZK
. If
p\neq 2
and
d=1
or
p=2
and
d=3
, then
\supp f
is contained in a group
ZK_{0}(a,b)
with
a+b>0
, so that we must in fact have
v(t_{1})=v(t_{2})=-k
. Suppose now that
d=0
or (
p=2
and
d=2
), and that
2k>c(\sigma)
. Then,
\supp f\subseteq Za(p^{c_{0}+d/2})^{-1}Ka(p^{c_{0}+d/2})
, so that
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})\geq-k-(c_{0}+d/2)
. But if either
v(t_{1})
or
v(t_{2})
were
>-k
, we would have that
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})=-2k
, which contradicts the assumption that
2k>c(\sigma)
.

Now, to show (
6.52
) it suffices to show that we can drop the restriction on the support of
\Phi
that appears in Theorems
6.20
and
6.29
for matrices of the form
n(t_{1})^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})\in\supp\Phi
.

Lemma
6.23
describes the support of
\Phi
in terms of the Iwasawa decomposition in Lemma
6.22
. To implement this, we write

(6.53)

n(t_{1})^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})=\begin{pmatrix}p^{-v(t_{2})-2k}&-p^{-2k}-t_{1}t_{2}\\
&t_{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
p^{-v(t_{2})}&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}p^{v(t_{2})}t_{2}^{-1}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}.

Lemma
6.23
breaks into cases depending on the size of
k
and
c(\sigma)
.

•

If
k\geq c(\sigma)-1
and
n(t_{1})^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})\in\supp\Phi
, then
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})\geq v(t_{2})-1
by Lemma
6.23
(i). Since
c_{0}\geq 1
and
v(t_{2})\geq-k
it follows that
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})\geq-k-c_{0}
.

•

If
k\leq c(\sigma)-2
and
2k\neq c(\sigma)
, then
v(t_{2})=-k
. So, when
n(t_{1})^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})\in\supp\Phi
we have
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})=-c(\sigma)
by Lemma
6.23
(ii).

•

If
2k=c(\sigma)
and
-v(t_{2})<c(\sigma)/2
, then Lemma
6.23
(ii) applies, so we get that
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})=-c(\sigma)
.

•

If
2k=c(\sigma)
and
-v(t_{2})=c(\sigma)/2
, then
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})=v(t_{2})-c(\sigma)/2
by Lemma
6.23
(iii), which is
\geq-c(\sigma)
.

We proceed by cases. If
p\neq 2
and
d=0
then
\Phi|_{ZK^{\prime}}=\Phi
by Theorem
6.20
so there is nothing to show.

Assume that
p\neq 2
and
d=1
. According to Theorem
6.20
we need to show that
n(t_{1})^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})\in Za(p^{c_{0}})^{-1}Ka(p^{c_{0}})
. Multiplying out and scaling by the square root of the determinant, it suffices to show that
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})\geq-c_{0}-k
.

•

If
k\geq c(\sigma)-1
, then we already showed
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})\geq-k-c_{0}
without casework.

•

If
k\leq c(\sigma)-2
and
2k\neq c(\sigma)
, then we showed
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})=-c(\sigma)
, which is
=-2c_{0}-1\geq-c_{0}-k
by (
6.8
) and since
p\neq 2
and
d=1
.

We move on to the
p=2
cases. Multiplying out
n(t_{1})^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})
and scaling by the square root of the determinant, according to Theorem
6.29
it suffices for the claim to show that
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})\geq-c_{0}-k-e+1
.

•

If
k\geq c(\sigma)-1
, then we already showed
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})\geq-k-c_{0}
without casework.

•

If
k\leq c(\sigma)-2
, then we showed
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})\geq-c(\sigma)
, which is
=-2c_{0}-d
by (
6.8
). We have by assumption that
k\geq v_{p}(y)
, with
v_{p}(y)
given by (
6.51
),
so that indeed
v(p^{-2k}+t_{1}t_{2})\geq-c_{0}-k-e+1
.

Note that when
2k>c(\sigma)
, the integral is restricted to
v(t_{1})=-k
and
v(t_{2})=-k
, but either integration or both may be trivially extended to
v(t_{i})\geq-k
and (
6.52
) remains valid.
∎

Now we open the matrix coefficient in (
6.52
) in the Whittaker model (
6.25
) to obtain

(6.54)

H_{p}(m,n;p^{k})\\
=f_{\xi}(1)\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(t_{2})\geq-k\\
v(t_{1})\geq-k\end{subarray}}\int_{F^{\times}}\overline{W}\left(a(y)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})\right)W\left(a(y)n(t_{1})\right)d^{\times}y\,\psi(-mt_{1}+nt_{2})\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2},

where
W
is a
L^{2}
-normalized newform in the Whittaker model of
\sigma
. Now we swap order of integration and evaluate the
t_{1}
integral. By the defining property of the Whittaker model we have
W\left(a(y)n(t_{1})\right)=\psi(yt_{1})W\left(a(y)\right)
, so

\int\limits_{v(t_{1})\geq-k}W\left(a(y)n(t_{1})\right)\psi(-mt_{1})\,dt_{1}=p^{k}W\left(a(y)\right)\delta_{y\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}}.

Since
W(a(y))=1_{1,0}(y)
, we already see that
H_{p}(m,n;p^{k})=0
unless
(mn,p)=1
, which we may freely assume for the rest of the proof. To compute
H_{p}(m,n;p^{k})
it suffices by Theorem
3.8
(4) to compute
H_{p}(m,1;p^{k})
. Collecting the above, we have

(6.55)

H_{p}(m,1;p^{k})=f_{\xi}(1)p^{k}\int\limits_{v(t_{2})\geq-k}\int\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\\
y\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\overline{W}\left(a(y)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})\right)\psi(t_{2})\,d^{\times}y\,\,dt_{2}.

Note that if
2k>c(\sigma)
then the condition
v(t_{2})\geq-k
in (
6.55
) may be replaced by
v(t_{2})=-k
.

The computation now breaks into cases depending on whether
2k>c(\sigma)
or
2k=c(\sigma)
.

Case
2k>c(\sigma)
:
We claim that the integrand in
y
is a constant function on
y\equiv m\pmod{p^{k}}
for fixed
t_{2}
. We would like to use
[
Hu18
, Prop. 2.12]
to accomplish this, so need to decompose the argument of
\overline{W}
according to Lemma
6.22
. Precisely, we have

\begin{pmatrix}y&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&-p^{-2k}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&t_{2}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}t_{2}&\\
&t_{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{y}{t_{2}p^{k}}&\frac{-y}{t_{2}p^{2k}}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
p^{k}&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}t_{2}^{-1}p^{-k}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix},

thus

W\left(\begin{pmatrix}y&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&-p^{-2k}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&t_{2}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\right)=\psi\left(-\frac{y}{t_{2}p^{2k}}\right)W\left(\begin{pmatrix}\frac{y}{t_{2}p^{k}}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
p^{k}&1\end{pmatrix}\right).

By
[
Hu18
, Prop. 2.12]
this is indeed a constant function of
y
once we restrict to
y\equiv m\pmod{p^{k}}
. Collecting these calculations, we have proven the following.

Lemma 6.49
.

Suppose that
k>c(\sigma)/2
. If
(m,p)\neq 1
then
H_{p}(m,1;p^{k})=0
and if
(m,p)=1
then

(6.56)

H_{p}(m,1;p^{k})=(1-p^{-1})^{-1}f_{\xi}(1)\int\limits_{v(t)=-k}\overline{W}\left(a(mp^{-2k})wn(t)\right)\psi(t)dt,

where
W
is an
L^{2}
-normalized newform in the Whittaker model of
\sigma
and
w=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-1\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

To continue the evaluation of the Kloosterman sum, we need to substitute in an expression for the Whittaker function. There are at least two different choices. One is to use minimal vectors as in
[
Hu24
]
. Another choice is to use results of Assing, namely
[
Ass19
, Lem. 3.1]
, which is the path that we pursue in this paper.

We use explicit expressions for the newform in the Whittaker model due to Assing
[
Ass19
]
. Following the notation after (1.4) in loc. cit.  let

(6.57)

g_{t,l,v}=\begin{pmatrix}0&p^{t}\\
-1&-vp^{-l}\end{pmatrix}

and note from the paragraph following (1.3) that Assing normalizes the additive Haar measure so that the total volume of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
is
p^{-\frac{d}{2}}
whereas we have taken the volume of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
to be 1. Now, Assing’s Lemma 3.1 asserts that (the
\Omega^{t/f}
there equals
\varpi_{E}^{-ke_{E}}
in our situation)

(6.58)

W\left(a(mp^{-2k})wn(t)\right)=W\left(g_{-2k,k,\frac{tp^{k}}{m}}\right)=\gamma p^{k-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}}\xi^{-1}(x)\psi(\Tr(x)p^{-k}+\frac{t}{m}\Nm(x))\,dx,

where
\gamma
is as in
[
JL70
, Lem. 1.2(iv)]
. In particular,
|\gamma|=1
and its value only depends on
E
.

Using (
6.58
) in (
6.56
), we have

(6.59)

H_{p}(m,1;p^{k})=\frac{f_{\xi}(1)}{\overline{\gamma}(1-p^{-1})}p^{k-\frac{d}{2}}\int\limits_{v(t)=-k}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}}\xi(x)\psi(-\Tr(x)p^{-k}-\frac{t}{m}\Nm(x))\,dx\,\psi(t)dt.

Now we swap order of integration and execute the integral in
t
:

(6.60)

H_{p}(m,1;p^{k})=\overline{\gamma}(1-p^{-1})^{-1}f_{\xi}(1)p^{k-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}}\xi(x)\psi(-\Tr(x)p^{-k})\\
\Big(\int\limits_{v(t)\geq-k}-\int\limits_{v(t)\geq-k+1}\Big)\psi\Big(\Big(1-\frac{\Nm(x)}{m}\Big)t\Big)\,dt\,dx.

Then for the
t
integral on the smaller domain we have

(6.61)

\int_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}}\xi(x)\psi(-\Tr(x)p^{-k})\int\limits_{v(t)\geq-k+1}\psi\Big(\Big(1-\frac{\Nm(x)}{m}\Big)t\Big)\,dt\,dx\\
=p^{k-1}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}}\xi(x)\psi(-\Tr(x)p^{-k})\delta_{\Nm(x)\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k-1}}}\,dx.

Our goal is to show that (
6.61
) vanishes.

Let
k^{\prime}=k-1-\lfloor d/2\rfloor.
Set
x=x_{0}+\Delta
with
v_{E}(\Delta)\geq ek^{\prime}
. Note by
[
BH06
, 41.2 Prop.(1)]
that
\Nm(x)\equiv m\pmod{p^{k-1}}
if and only if
\Nm(x_{0})\equiv m\pmod{p^{k-1}}
. Also, since
2k>c(\sigma)
, we have
k\geq c_{0}+\lceil\frac{d+1}{2}\rceil
, so that
k^{\prime}\geq c_{0}
. Therefore,
\xi(x)=\xi(x_{0})
. Collecting these facts, we have
we have that the integral in (
6.61
) equals

(6.62)

\sum_{x_{0}\in(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p^{k^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}_{E})^{\times}}\xi(x_{0})\psi(-\Tr(x_{0})p^{-k})\delta_{\Nm(x_{0})\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k-1}}}\int_{\Delta\in p^{k^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\psi(-\Tr(\Delta)p^{-k})\,d\Delta.

The additive character
\psi\circ\Tr
of
E
has conductor
-d
by (
6.2
), and
v_{E}(\Delta p^{-k})=e(k^{\prime}-k)<-d
, so that the interior integral in (
6.62
) vanishes.

Therefore, in the case that
2k>c(\sigma)
we conclude the theorem statement from (
6.60
) by one more application of orthogonality of additive characters.

Case
2k=c(\sigma)
:
This case can only occur when
E/F
is unramified, or when
p=2
and
d=2
.
In this case, the condition
2k=c(\sigma)
is equivalent to
k=c_{0}+d/2
.

We pick up the calculation at (
6.55
), and use Atkin-Lehner theory to continue.
Indeed, let
\delta_{\pi}
denote the eigenvalue of the newform
\varphi_{0}
in an
\pi\in\overline{G}(F)^{\wedge}
under the Atkin-Lehner operator:

(6.63)

\pi\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
p^{c(\pi)}&\end{smallmatrix}\right)\varphi_{0}=\delta_{\pi}\varphi_{0}.

If the central character of
\pi
is trivial, one has
\delta_{\pi}=\pm 1
. Applying this in the Whittaker model of
\sigma
(in which
W
is an
L^{2}
-normalized newform), we have

W\left(a(y)\begin{pmatrix}0&-p^{-2k}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}n(t)\right)=\delta_{\sigma}W\left(a(y)\begin{pmatrix}0&-p^{-2k}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}n(t)\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
p^{c(\sigma)}&\end{pmatrix}\right).

Write
i:=-v(t)\leq k
. Now one can verify that

a(y)\begin{pmatrix}0&-p^{-2k}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}n(t)\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
p^{c(\sigma)}&\end{pmatrix}=a(-y/tp^{i})\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
p^{{c(\sigma)}-i}&1\end{pmatrix}a(tp^{i})\\
\in B\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
p^{{c(\sigma)}-i}&1\end{pmatrix}K_{1}(p^{c(\sigma)}).

We thus get that for
i=-v(t)\leq k

(6.64)

W\left(a(y)\begin{pmatrix}0&-p^{-2k}\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}n(t)\right)=\delta_{\sigma}W\left(a(-y/tp^{i})\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
p^{{c(\sigma)}-i}&1\end{pmatrix}\right).

By
[
Hu17
, Prop. 2.12]
, this is
U(i)
-invariant in
y
.
Inserting (
6.64
) in (
6.55
) we get

(6.65)

H_{p}(m,1;p^{k})=f_{\xi}(1)p^{c_{0}}\delta_{\sigma}\int\limits_{v(t)=-i\geq-k}\int\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\\
y\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\overline{W}\left(a(-y/tp^{i})\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
p^{{c(\sigma)}-i}&1\end{pmatrix}\right)\psi(t)\,d^{\times}y\,\,dt\\
=(1-p^{-1})^{-1}f_{\xi}(1)\delta_{\sigma}\int\limits_{v(t)=-i\geq-k}\overline{W}\left(a(-m/tp^{i})\begin{pmatrix}1&\\
p^{{c(\sigma)}-i}&1\end{pmatrix}\right)\psi(t)\,dt,

where the 2nd line follows from the
U(i)
-invariance, since
i\leq k
.

With
W
a normalized newform in the Whittaker model as before, set

W^{(i)}(y):=W\left(\begin{pmatrix}y&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
p^{i}&1\end{pmatrix}\right).

The following Lemma is a mild extension of
[
HS20
, Lem. 5.7]
.

Lemma 6.50
.

Let
\pi
be a dihedral supercuspidal representation corresponding to
\Ind_{E}^{F}\xi
by the LLC. Let
W
be an
L^{2}
-normalized newform in its Whittaker model.
When
i\geq c(\pi)/2
and
v(y)=0
,

(6.66)

W^{(i)}(y)=\frac{\delta_{\pi}\gamma}{\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}(1)}p^{\frac{c(\pi)-d}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}}\xi^{-1}(x)\psi\left(-\frac{\Nm(x)}{yp^{c(\pi)-i}}+\frac{\Tr(x)}{p^{c(\pi)/2}}\right)\,d^{\times}x,

where
\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}(1)=(1-q_{E}^{-1})^{-1}
and
q_{E}
is the cardinality of the residue field of
E
.

Proof.

Let us write
c=c(\pi)
within this proof.
We have

\displaystyle W^{(i)}(y)

\displaystyle=W\Big(\begin{pmatrix}y&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&p^{i}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}\Big)

\displaystyle=W\Big(\begin{pmatrix}yp^{-c}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&p^{i-c}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
p^{c}&\end{pmatrix}\Big)

\displaystyle=\delta_{\pi}W\Big(\begin{pmatrix}yp^{-c}&\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}&1\\
1&\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&p^{i-c}\\
&1\end{pmatrix}\Big)=\delta_{\pi}W(g_{-c,c-i,-y^{-1}}),

where
\delta_{\pi}
is the eigenvalue of the Atkin-Lehner involution and
g_{t,l,v}
is as in (
6.57
). Note that for
W
the newform in the Whittaker model of a supercuspidal representation, we have
\|W\|_{2}=W(1)=1
, so that Assing’s normalization matches the normalization here. Now we apply the middle case of
[
Ass19
, Lem. 3.1]
, noting that Assing normalizes the measure on
\mathcal{O}_{E}
to have total volume
p^{-d/2}
, with
n=c
in both the
i>c/2
and
i=c/2
cases to get

W^{(i)}(y)=\delta_{\pi}\gamma p^{\frac{c-d}{2}}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}}\xi^{-1}(x)\psi_{E}(\varpi_{E}^{-ce/2}x)\psi\left(-\frac{p^{i-c}}{y}\Nm(x)\right)dx.

Converting additive to multiplicative measure yields the result.
∎

Applying Lemma
6.50
to (
6.65
) we get

(6.67)

\frac{f_{\xi}(1)}{(1-p^{-1})}\frac{\delta_{\sigma}^{2}\overline{\gamma}p^{\frac{c(\sigma)-d}{2}}}{\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}(1)}\int\limits_{v(t)=-i\geq-k}\psi(t)\int_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}}\xi(x)\psi\left(-\frac{t}{m}\Nm(x)-\Tr(xp^{-c(\sigma)/2})\right)\,d^{\times}x\,dt.

Applying orthogonality of additive characters, we obtain

(6.68)

H_{p}(m,1;p^{k})=(1-p^{-1})^{-1}f_{\xi}(1)\frac{\overline{\gamma}}{\zeta_{\mathfrak{p}}(1)}p^{c_{0}+k}\int\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}\\
\Nm(x)=m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\xi(x)\psi(-\Tr(x)p^{-k})\,d^{\times}x\\
=\overline{\gamma}(1-p^{-1})^{-1}f_{\xi}(1)p^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p^{k}\mathcal{O}_{E})^{\times}\\
\Nm(x)=m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\xi(x)\psi(-p^{-k}\Tr(x))

after converting from multiplicative to additive measure.
∎

Under the same hypotheses as Theorem
6.45
, we have that the supercuspidal Kloosterman sums degenerate into classical Kloosterman sums for
k\geq c(\sigma)
.

Proposition 6.51
.

For
k\geq c(\sigma)
and
(m,n,p)=1
we have

H_{p}(m,n,p^{k})=f_{\xi}(1)\zeta_{p}(1)S(m,n,p^{k}).

Proof.

We use the expression in Lemma
6.48
for
H(m,n;p^{k})
, that is

H_{p}(m,n;p^{k})=f_{\xi}(1)\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(t_{2})=-k\\
v(t_{1})=-k\end{subarray}}\overline{\Phi}\left(n(t_{1})^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&-p^{-2k}\\
1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)n(t_{2})\right)\psi(-mt_{1}+nt_{2})\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}.

Now, using the matrix decomposition in (
6.53
), we get that this is equal to

f_{\xi}(1)\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(t_{2})=-k\\
v(t_{1})=-k\end{subarray}}\overline{\Phi}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}(p^{k}t_{2})^{-1}&\frac{-p^{-2k}-t_{1}t_{2}}{t_{2}}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\psi(-mt_{1}+nt_{2})\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}.

By Proposition 3.1 of
[
Hu18
]
this is

(6.69)

f_{\xi}(1)\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(t_{2})=v(t_{1})=-k\\
v\left(\frac{-p^{-2k}-t_{1}t_{2}}{t_{2}}\right)\geq 0\end{subarray}}\psi(-mt_{1}+nt_{2})\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}-\frac{f_{\xi}(1)}{p-1}\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(t_{2})=v(t_{1})=-k\\
v\left(\frac{-p^{-2k}-t_{1}t_{2}}{t_{2}}\right)=-1\end{subarray}}\psi(-mt_{1}+nt_{2})\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}.

The first of the two terms in (
6.69
) equals

f_{\xi}(1)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\\
v(-1-t_{1}t_{2})\geq k\end{subarray}}\psi\left(\frac{-mt_{1}+nt_{2}}{p^{k}}\right)=f_{\xi}(1)S(m,n,p^{k}).

The second term (including the minus sign) in (
6.69
) equals

-\frac{f_{\xi}(1)}{p-1}\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(t_{2})=v(t_{1})=-k\\
v\left(\frac{-p^{-2k}-t_{1}t_{2}}{t_{2}}\right)\geq-1\end{subarray}}\psi(-mt_{1}+nt_{2})\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}+\frac{f_{\xi}(1)}{p-1}\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(t_{2})=v(t_{1})=-k\\
v\left(\frac{-p^{-2k}-t_{1}t_{2}}{t_{2}}\right)\geq 0\end{subarray}}\psi(-mt_{1}+nt_{2})\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}.

The second of these again equals
\frac{f_{\xi}(1)}{p-1}S(m,n;p^{k})
, while the first equals

-\frac{f_{\xi}(1)}{p-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\\
v(-1-t_{1}t_{2})\geq k-1\end{subarray}}\psi\left(\frac{-mt_{1}+nt_{2}}{p^{k}}\right)=-\frac{f_{\xi}(1)}{p-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\\
t_{1}t_{2}\equiv 1\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k-1}}\end{subarray}}\psi\left(\frac{mt_{1}+nt_{2}}{p^{k}}\right).

Since
k\geq 2
, writing
t_{i}=t_{i,0}+p^{k-1}t_{i,1}
, this is

-\frac{f_{\xi}(1)}{p-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1,0},t_{2,0}\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k-1}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\\
t_{1}t_{2}\equiv 1\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k-1}}\end{subarray}}\psi\left(\frac{mt_{1,0}+nt_{2,0}}{p^{k-1}}\right)\sum_{t_{1,1},t_{2,1}\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}}\psi\left(\frac{mt_{1,1}+nt_{2,1}}{p}\right)=0,

since
(m,n,p)=1
.
∎

6.5.
p
-adic stationary phase

Let
\alpha_{0}
be a normalized minimal element as in Definition
6.2
, which we moreover assume to have
\Tr(\alpha_{0})=0
when
p
is odd and to be given by Lemma
6.31
when
p=2
. Let
D=(\Tr(\alpha_{0}))^{2}-4\Nm(\alpha_{0})
and
d=v(D)
.

Lemma 6.52
.

Suppose
\sigma
is a trivial central character dihedral supercuspidal representation of
\GL_{2}(F)
corresponding to
\Ind_{E}^{F}\xi
under the LLC, and
c(\sigma)\geq 5
if
p=2
. Suppose
k\geq\max(\lceil c(\sigma)/2\rceil,2)
. Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
with
\Nm(u_{0})=m\pmod{p^{k}}
and write
u_{0}=a+b\alpha_{0}
with
a,b\in\mathcal{O}
.
The integral
R_{k,\xi}(b)
given by

R_{k,\xi}(b)=\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{E}(du)\geq ek/2\\
\Nm(u_{0}+du)\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\xi(1+\frac{du}{u_{0}})\psi_{E}(-du/p^{k})\,d(du)

vanishes if
p=2
,
d=0
and
v(a)>0
. Suppose now that
v(a)=0
if
p=2
and
d=0
.

(1)

If
v(2b\Nm(\alpha_{0})+a\Tr(\alpha_{0}))<\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
, then

R_{k,\xi}(b)=p^{-\lceil\frac{3k-d}{2}\rceil}\delta\left(bD\equiv 2\Tr\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi}p^{k}\pmod{p^{\lfloor\frac{k+d}{2}\rfloor}}\right),

and

(2)

if
v(2b\Nm(\alpha_{0})+a\Tr(\alpha_{0}))\geq\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
, then

R_{k,\xi}(b)=p^{-\lceil\frac{3k-d}{2}\rceil}\delta\left(\lceil\frac{k-(e-1)}{2}\rceil\geq c_{0}\right).

Proof.

Write
du=da+db\alpha_{0}
,
da,db\in\mathcal{O}
. Since
v_{E}(du)\geq ek/2
, we have (Lemma
6.3
) that

\min(ev(da),ev(db)+e-1)\geq ek/2,\quad\text{ i.e. }\quad v(da)\geq k/2,\text{ and }v(db)\geq\frac{k-(e-1)}{2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\Nm(u)

\displaystyle=(a+da)^{2}+\Tr(\alpha_{0})(a+da)(b+db)+\Nm(\alpha_{0})(b+db)^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv m+2ada+2\Nm(\alpha_{0})bdb+\Tr(\alpha_{0})(adb+bda)\pmod{p^{k}}.

So, the condition
\Nm(u_{0}+du)=m\pmod{p^{k}}
on the integration is equivalent to

(2a+\Tr(\alpha_{0})b)da+(2b\Nm(\alpha_{0})+\Tr(\alpha_{0})a)db\equiv 0\pmod{p^{k}}.

Set
a^{\prime}=2a+\Tr(\alpha_{0})b
and
b^{\prime}=2b\Nm(\alpha_{0})+\Tr(\alpha_{0})a
.
Since

v_{E}(\frac{du}{u_{0}})\geq\frac{ek}{2}\geq\frac{e}{2}(c_{0}+\lceil\frac{d}{2}\rceil)\geq\frac{c(\xi)}{2},

we have for any
\alpha_{\xi}\in E
with
v_{E}(\alpha_{\xi})=-c(\xi)+c(\psi_{E})
corresponding to
\xi
by the Postnikov Lemma
6.1
that

(6.70)

R_{k,\xi}(b)=\int_{v(da)\geq k/2}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(db)\geq(k-(e-1))/2\\
a^{\prime}da+b^{\prime}db\equiv 0\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\psi_{E}\Big(\Big(\frac{\alpha_{\xi}}{u_{0}}-\frac{1}{p^{k}}\Big)(da+db\alpha_{0})\Big)\,d(da)\,d(db).

We have

\psi_{E}(-(da+db\alpha_{0})p^{-k})=\psi(-2p^{-k}da)\psi(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})p^{-k}db)

and

\frac{\alpha_{\xi}}{u_{0}}(da+db\alpha_{0})=\alpha_{\xi}\frac{ada+\alpha_{0}adb+\overline{\alpha_{0}}bda+\Nm(\alpha_{0})bdb}{\Nm(u_{0})}.

Note that
\xi(x)
is trivial on norms from
E^{\times}
since
\sigma
has trivial central character, so that
\xi(\overline{x})=\xi(x)^{-1}
and thus
\Tr(\alpha_{\xi})=0
. So,

(6.71)

\Tr\left(\frac{\alpha_{\xi}}{u_{0}}(da+db\alpha_{0})\right)=\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{adb-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}.

Thus, the integral in (
6.70
) is equal to

(6.72)

\int_{v(da)\geq k/2}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\\
\times\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(db)\geq(k-(e-1))/2\\
a^{\prime}da+b^{\prime}db\equiv 0\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\psi\Big(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{db}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi(\Tr\Big(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{adb}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(da)\,d(db).

Note that
{v(}\Tr(\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi}){)}=-c_{0}

by a case check using e.g.
[
BH06
, 41.2 Prop.]
when
p=2
.

We now restrict to case (1), i.e. we have the hypothesis that
v(b^{\prime})<\lfloor\frac{k-(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
. We split the
da
integral into two ranges:
v(da)\geq k-v(a^{\prime})
and
k/2\leq v(da)<k-v(a^{\prime})
.
Consider the first one:

(6.73)

\int_{v(da)\geq\max(k-v(a^{\prime}),k/2)}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\\
\times\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(db)\geq(k-(e-1))/2\\
a^{\prime}da+b^{\prime}db\equiv 0\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\psi\Big(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{db}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{adb}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(da)\,d(db).

In this case, the congruence
a^{\prime}da+b^{\prime}db\equiv 0\pmod{p^{k}}
is equivalent to
v(db)\geq k-v(b^{\prime})
.
The integral becomes

(6.74)

\int_{v(da)\geq\max(k-v(a^{\prime}),k/2)}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(da)\\
\times\int_{v(db)\geq k-v(b^{\prime})}\psi\Big(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{db}{p^{k}})\psi(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{adb}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(db).

The integral in
db
is

p^{-(k-v(b^{\prime}))}\delta(\frac{a\Tr(\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi})}{\Nm(u_{0})}\equiv\frac{\Tr\alpha_{0}}{p^{k}}\pmod{p^{-(k-v(b^{\prime}))}}).

Now consider the other part of the
da
integral, i.e.

(6.75)

\int_{k/2\leq v(da)<k-v(a^{\prime})}\psi(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}})\psi(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})})\\
\times\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(db)\geq(k-(e-1))/2\\
a^{\prime}da+b^{\prime}db\equiv 0\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\psi\Big(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{db}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{adb}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(da)\,d(db).

In this case, the congruence
a^{\prime}da+b^{\prime}db\equiv 0\pmod{p^{k}}
implies the condition
v(a^{\prime}da)=v(b^{\prime}db)
, in the presence of which the condition
v(db)\geq(k-(e-1))/2
is equivalent to

v(da)\geq\frac{k}{2}+\max(v(b^{\prime})-v(a^{\prime})-\frac{e-1}{2},0).

We consider
da
to be a fixed variable and write the congruence condition for
db
as

db=-\frac{a^{\prime}da}{b^{\prime}}+p^{k-v(b^{\prime})}dx,

where
dx\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
. The result of these transformations is that the integral in (
6.75
) is

(6.76)

p^{-(k-v(b^{\prime}))}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(da)<k-v(a^{\prime})\\
v(da)\geq\frac{k}{2}+\max(v(b^{\prime})-v(a^{\prime})-\frac{e-1}{2},0)\end{subarray}}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\psi\Big(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{-a^{\prime}da}{b^{\prime}p^{k}}\Big)\\
\times\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{a(-a^{\prime}da)}{b^{\prime}\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(da)\int_{\mathcal{O}}\psi\Big(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{p^{k-v(b^{\prime})}dx}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{a(p^{k-v(b^{\prime})}dx)}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(dx).

The integral in
dx
is

\delta(\frac{a\Tr(\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi})}{\Nm(u_{0})}\equiv\frac{\Tr\alpha_{0}}{p^{k}}\pmod{p^{-(k-v(b^{\prime}))}}).

Putting the cases back together, we have that
R_{k,\xi}(b)
is equal to

(6.77)

p^{-(k-v(b^{\prime}))}\delta(\frac{a\Tr(\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi})}{\Nm(u_{0})}\equiv\frac{\Tr\alpha_{0}}{p^{k}}\pmod{p^{-(k-v(b^{\prime}))}})

times

(6.78)

\int_{v(da)\geq\max(k-v(a^{\prime}),k/2)}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\\
+\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}v(da)<k-v(a^{\prime})\\
v(da)\geq\frac{k}{2}+\max(v(b^{\prime})-v(a^{\prime})-\frac{e-1}{2},0)\end{subarray}}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\psi\Big(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{-a^{\prime}da}{b^{\prime}p^{k}}\Big)\\
\times\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{a(-a^{\prime}da)}{b^{\prime}\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(da).

Under the condition in (
6.77
) we can combine the integrals in (
6.78
) as

\int_{v(da)\geq\frac{k}{2}+\max(v(b^{\prime})-v(a^{\prime})-\frac{e-1}{2},0)}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\\
\times\psi\Big(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{-a^{\prime}da}{b^{\prime}p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{a(-a^{\prime}da)}{b^{\prime}\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(da).

Note that

b+\frac{aa^{\prime}}{b^{\prime}}=\frac{2\Nm(u_{0})}{b^{\prime}},

so
R_{k,\xi}(b)
is equal to the expression in (
6.77
) times

(6.79)

\int_{v(da)\geq\frac{k}{2}+\max(v(b^{\prime})-v(a^{\prime})-\frac{e-1}{2},0)}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{a^{\prime}da}{b^{\prime}p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(-2\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{da}{b^{\prime}}\Big)\,d(da).

Note that

\frac{-2}{p^{k}}+\frac{\Tr(\alpha_{0})a^{\prime}}{b^{\prime}p^{k}}=\frac{1}{b^{\prime}p^{k}}((\Tr\alpha_{0})^{2}-4\Nm(\alpha_{0}))b=\frac{bD}{b^{\prime}p^{k}},

so that the integral in (
6.79
) equals

p^{-\lceil\frac{k}{2}+\max(v(b^{\prime})-v(a^{\prime})-\frac{e-1}{2},0)\rceil}\delta\left(\frac{1}{b^{\prime}p^{k}}(bD-2\Tr\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi}p^{k})\equiv 0\pmod{p^{-\lceil\frac{k}{2}+\max(v(b^{\prime})-v(a^{\prime})-\frac{e-1}{2},0)\rceil}}\right).

We have the following table of cases.

Case

v(a^{\prime})

v(b^{\prime})

v(b^{\prime})-v(a^{\prime})-\frac{e-1}{2}

p=2,

d=0,v(a)=0

\geq 0

=0

\leq 0

p=2
,
d=0,v(a)>0

=0

\geq 1

v(b^{\prime})

d=3

=1

=v(b)+2

v(b^{\prime})-3/2

d=2

\geq 1

\leq 0

p\neq 2
,
d=0
,
v(a)=0

=0

,
d=0
,
v(a)>0

\geq 1

=0

\leq 0

=0

=v(b)+1

Table 3.

Note that we have uniformly that

-\max(v(b^{\prime})-v(a^{\prime})-\frac{e-1}{2},0)+v(b^{\prime})=\frac{d}{2}.

Collecting these computations,

(6.80)

R_{k,\xi}(b)=p^{-\lceil\frac{3k-d}{2}\rceil}\delta(bD\equiv 2\Tr\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi}p^{k}\pmod{p^{\lfloor\frac{k+d}{2}\rfloor}})\delta(\frac{a\Tr(\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi})}{\Nm u_{0}}\equiv\frac{\Tr\alpha_{0}}{p^{k}}\pmod{p^{-k+v(b^{\prime})}}).

Note that
\frac{\Tr\alpha_{0}}{p^{k}}\equiv 0\pmod{p^{-k+v(b^{\prime})}})
in every case except
p=2
,
d=0
,
v(a)>0
. However, in that exceptional case
v(\frac{a\Tr(\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi})}{\Nm u_{0}})>v(\frac{\Tr\alpha_{0}}{p^{k}})
, so that the latter congruence of (
6.80
) can never be satisfied. Thus
R_{k,\xi}(b)
is identically 0 if
p=2
,
d=0
and
v(a)>0
.

Excluding now the case
p=2
,
d=0
,
v(a)>0
, the expression in (
6.80
) simplifies to

p^{-\lceil\frac{3k-d}{2}\rceil}\delta(bD\equiv 2\Tr\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi}p^{k}\pmod{p^{\lfloor\frac{k+d}{2}\rfloor}})\delta(v(a)-c_{0}\geq-k+v(b^{\prime})).

If
p=2
,
d=0
,
v(a)=0
, the condition
\delta(v(a)-c_{0}\geq-k+v(b^{\prime}))
is trivially satisfied by the hypothesis
k\geq\lceil c(\sigma)/2\rceil
of the proposition.

Now excluding the unramified
p=2
case, we have that the congruence condition
bD\equiv 2\Tr\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi}p^{k}\pmod{p^{\lfloor\frac{k+d}{2}\rfloor}}
implies that
v(a)-c_{0}\geq-k+v(b^{\prime})
, so in fact the latter condition can be omitted. The result is: if
v(b^{\prime})<\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
then

R_{k,\xi}(b)=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }p=2,d=0,v(a)>0\\
p^{-\lceil\frac{3k-d}{2}\rceil}\delta(bD\equiv 2\Tr\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi}p^{k}\pmod{p^{\lfloor\frac{k+d}{2}\rfloor}})&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

Now consider case (2), i.e. that
v(b^{\prime})\geq\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
. We pick up the calculation at (
6.72
). In this case, the congruence condition
a^{\prime}da+b^{\prime}db\equiv 0\pmod{p^{k}}
becomes just
v(da)\geq k-v(a^{\prime})
, so the two integrals separate, i.e. we have that

R_{k,\xi}(b)=\int_{v(da)\geq k-v(a^{\prime})}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(da)\\
\times\int_{v(db)\geq(k-(e-1))/2}\psi\Big(-\Tr(\alpha_{0})\frac{db}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{adb}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(db).

First assume that
p=2
,
d=0
, and
v(a)>0
. In this case we have
v(\frac{a\Tr(\alpha_{0}\alpha_{\xi})}{\Nm u_{0}})>v(\frac{\Tr\alpha_{0}}{p^{k}})
, so that the
db
integral vanishes for all
k,\xi,u_{0}
.

Now, excluding this case, it remains to consider only the cases
d=3
, (
p\neq 2
,
d=0
,
v(a)=0
), and (
p\neq 2
,
d=1
), since only these cases may have
v(b^{\prime})\geq 1
, and by assumption
v(b^{\prime})\geq\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
. (Note, the case
d=2
,
c_{0}=1
,
k=2
is excluded by the hypothesis that
c(\sigma)\geq 5
when
p=2
.)
All of these cases conveniently have
\Tr\alpha_{0}=0
and
v(a^{\prime})=v(2)
,
so

R_{k,\xi}(b)=\int_{v(da)\geq k-v(2)}\psi\Big(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}}\Big)\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(da)\\
\times\int_{v(db)\geq(k-(e-1))/2}\psi\Big(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{adb}{\Nm(u_{0})}\Big)\,d(db).

Note that
\psi(-\frac{2da}{p^{k}})=1
, and since

k-c_{0}\geq\lceil\frac{d}{2}\rceil\geq v(2)\geq v(2)-v(b),

we have
\psi(\Tr(\alpha_{\xi}\alpha_{0})\frac{-bda}{\Nm(u_{0})})=1
as well. Thus the
da
integral is equal to
p^{-k+v(2)}
. Meanwhile, the
db
integral equals

p^{-\lceil\frac{k-(e-1)}{2}\rceil}\delta(\lceil\frac{k-(e-1)}{2}\rceil\geq c_{0}).

So, the whole integral, under the hypothesis
v(b^{\prime})\geq\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
is

\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }p=2,d=0,v(a)>0\\
p^{-\lceil\frac{3k-d}{2}\rceil}\delta\left(\lceil\frac{k-(e-1)}{2}\rceil\geq c_{0}\right)&\text{ otherwise. }\end{cases}

∎

Lemma 6.53
.

Let
n\geq 1
. Then
y+z\alpha_{0}\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}U_{E}(n)
if and only if
v(y)=0
and
v_{E}(z\alpha_{0})\geq n
.

Proof.

If: Write
y+z\alpha_{0}=y(1+\frac{z}{y}\alpha_{0})
, which we are allowed since
v(y)=0
. Then
v_{E}(\frac{z}{y}\alpha_{0})=v_{E}(z\alpha_{0})\geq n
by assumption. So
y+z\alpha_{0}\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}U_{E}(n)
. Only if: by hypothesis there exists
s,a,b\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
with
v(s)=0
,
v_{E}(a-1+b\alpha_{0})\geq n
and
y+z\alpha_{0}=s(a+b\alpha_{0})
. By Lemma
6.3
, we have
\min(v_{E}(a-1),v_{E}(b\alpha_{0}))\geq n
. From these it follows that
v(a-1)\geq n/e
,
v_{E}(b\alpha_{0})\geq n
,
y=sa
, and
z\alpha_{0}=sb\alpha_{0}
. Since
n\geq 1
, we have
v(a)=0
. Thus,
v(y)=0
and
v_{E}(z\alpha_{0})=v_{E}(b\alpha_{0})\geq n
.
∎

Set

(6.81)

I_{\xi}(m,p^{k})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p^{k}\mathcal{O}_{E})^{\times}\\
\Nm(u)\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\xi(u)\psi(-\Tr(u)p^{-k}),

so that the supercuspidal Kloosterman sum
H(m,1,p^{k})
associated to
\Ind_{E}^{F}\xi
is equal to
\delta_{p}\overline{\gamma}p^{-d/2}I_{\xi}(m,p^{k})
if
(m,p)=1
and
k\geq c_{0}+\lceil d/2\rceil
and
0
otherwise, see Theorem
6.45
.

Let
\xi
be a character of
E^{\times}
, and for
0\leq n\leq c(\xi)
, recall (
6.10
) the neighborhood
\xi[n]
of characters around
\xi
, and for
0\leq i\leq n
the equivalence relation
\sim_{i}
on
\xi[n]
.

Proposition 6.54
.

Set
i=1
if the
E
on which
\xi
is defined is the unramified quadratic extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{2}
and
i=0
otherwise. Suppose
i\leq n<c(\xi)
and
k\geq 2
. If
k\geq c_{0}+\lceil d/2\rceil-i+\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor
, then

(6.82)

\frac{1}{[\xi[n]:\xi[i]]}\sum_{\xi_{1}\in\xi[n]/\sim_{i}}I_{\xi_{1}}(m,p^{k})=I_{\xi}(m,p^{k}).

Proof.

We have for any
0\leq i\leq n\leq c(\xi)

\xi[n]/\sim_{i}=\{\xi_{1}\in(U_{E}(i))^{\wedge}:c(\xi_{1}\xi^{-1})\leq n,\,\xi_{1}|_{\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}}=\xi|_{\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}}\}=\xi\{\theta\in(U_{E}(i))^{\wedge}:c(\theta)\leq n,\,\theta|_{\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}}=1\}.

So, for
u\in U_{E}(i)
, we have

(6.83)

\frac{1}{[\xi[n]:\xi[i]]}\sum_{\xi_{1}\in\xi[n]/\sim_{i}}\xi_{1}(u)=\frac{1}{[1[n]:1[i]]}\sum_{\theta\in 1[n]/1[i]}\xi(u)\theta(u)=\xi(u)\delta_{u\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}U_{E}(n)}.

We get for
i
as in the statement of the proposition (using Remark
6.47
when
i=1
) that

(6.84)

\frac{1}{[\xi[n]:\xi[i]]}\sum_{\xi_{1}\in\xi[n]/\sim_{i}}I_{\xi_{1}}(m,p^{k})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p^{k}\mathcal{O}_{E})^{\times}\\
\Nm(u)\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\\
u\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}U_{E}(n)\end{subarray}}\xi(u)\psi(-\Tr(u)p^{-k}).

So, to prove the proposition, it suffices to show that the right hand side of (
6.84
) is equal to the right hand side of (
6.82
).
Note that these clearly match if
n=i
by Theorem
6.45
and Remark
6.47
, so we may freely assume that
1\leq n<c(\xi)
for the remainder of the proof.

First suppose that
k\geq c_{0}+\lceil d/2\rceil-i+\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor
and work from the right hand side of (
6.82
). Note that
c_{0}+\lceil d/2\rceil-i+\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor=c_{0}+d-v(2)+\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor.

Writing
u=u_{0}+du
with
v_{E}(du)\geq ek/2
and
R_{k,\xi}(b)
for the integral in Lemma
6.52
, we have

(6.85)

I_{\xi}(m,p^{k})=p^{2k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{0}\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}/U_{E}(\lceil ek/2\rceil)\\
\Nm(u_{0})\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\end{subarray}}\xi(u_{0})\psi_{E}(-u_{0}p^{-k})R_{k,\xi}(b).

Here, and in similar situations below (e.g. (
6.94
)) the sum on the right hand side runs over

\{u_{0}\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}/U_{E}(\lceil ek/2\rceil):\exists\text{ a lift }\tilde{u}_{0}\in(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p^{k}\mathcal{O}_{E})^{\times}\text{ of }u_{0}\text{ with }\Nm(\tilde{u}_{0})\equiv m\negthickspace\negthickspace\negthickspace\pmod{p^{k}}\}.

Write
u_{0}=a+b\alpha_{0}
.
We claim that
\supp(R_{k,\xi}(b))\cap\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}U_{E}(n)
, so that (
6.85
) matches the right hand side of (
6.84
).

Set
b^{\prime}=2b\Nm(\alpha_{0})+a\Tr(\alpha_{0})
as in the proof of Lemma
6.52
. Suppose first that
v(b^{\prime})<\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
. Then, Lemma
6.52
(1) shows that
a+b\alpha_{0}\in\supp(R_{k,\xi}(b))
only if

v(b)\geq\min(\lfloor\frac{k-d}{2}\rfloor,k-c_{0}-d+v(2)).

The second of these two possibilities is
\geq\lfloor n/e\rfloor
by the case hypothesis, while for the first we have

\frac{k-d}{2}=\frac{k-d-c_{0}+v(2)}{2}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}-\frac{v(2)}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor+\frac{c_{0}}{2}-\frac{v(2)}{2}\geq\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor-\frac{v(2)}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\geq\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor.

Then,

v_{E}(b\alpha_{0})=ev(b)+(e-1)\geq e\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor+(e-1)\geq n.

Since
0=v_{E}(a+b\alpha_{0})=\min(v_{E}(a),v_{E}(b\alpha_{0}))
, we must have
v(a)=0
. By Lemma
6.53
, those
u_{0}=a+b\alpha_{0}\in\supp(R_{k,\xi}(b))\cap\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
with
v(b^{\prime})<\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
lie in
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}U_{E}(n)
.

Now suppose that
v(b^{\prime})\geq\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor
. We need some casework so refer to Table
3
. Since
\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor\geq 1
, and is
\geq 2
when
d=2
by the hypothesis that
c(\sigma)\geq 5
when
p=2
, we have that
\supp(R_{k,\xi}(b))
is only non-empty in the cases
d=3
, (
p\neq 2
,
d=0
,
v(a)=0
), and (
p\neq 2
,
d=1
). In these cases,
b^{\prime}
and
b
are related by
v(b^{\prime})=v(b)+e-1+v(2)
. So,

v(b)\geq\lfloor\frac{k+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor-(e-1)-v(2)

and

k\geq c_{0}+d+\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor-v(2)\geq 2\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor+d-v(2)+1,

so that

v(b)\geq\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor+\lfloor\frac{d-v(2)+1+(e-1)}{2}\rfloor-(e-1)-v(2)\geq\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor.

Therefore,
v_{E}(b\alpha_{0})\geq n
and so
\supp(R_{k,\xi}(b))\cap\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}U_{E}(n)
by Lemma
6.53
.
∎

Now write
\xi^{\prime}
for a twist-minimal character of
E^{\times}
for which there exists a character
\chi
of
F^{\times}
with
\xi=\xi^{\prime}\chi_{E}
, following section
6.3
. Recall that if
p\neq 2
or
d=3
, then we may take
\xi^{\prime}=\xi
and if
p=2
and
d=0
or
2
, then we have that
c(\xi^{\prime})=c(\xi)-1
, see Table
2
.

Proposition 6.55
.

Set
i=1
if the
E
on which
\xi
is defined is the unramified quadratic extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{2}
and
i=0
otherwise. Suppose
i\leq n<c(\xi^{\prime})
and
k\geq 2
. If
k<c_{0}+\lceil d/2\rceil-i+\lfloor\frac{n}{e}\rfloor
, then

(6.86)

\frac{1}{[\xi[n]:\xi[i]]}\sum_{\xi_{1}\in\xi[n]/\sim_{i}}I_{\xi_{1}}(m,p^{k})\\
=0.

Proof.

The conditions for
n
can be rewritten as

e\left(k-c_{0}-\lceil d/2\rceil+i+1\right)\leq n<c(\xi^{\prime})

For such
n
to exist, we have in view of Theorem
6.45
,

(6.87)

\lceil c(\sigma)/2\rceil\leq k<c_{0}+\lceil d/2\rceil-i-1+c(\xi^{\prime})/e

We first reduce to the case

(6.88)

n=n_{0}:=e\left(k-c_{0}-\lceil d/2\rceil+i+1\right).

Indeed if the result is true for
n_{0}
, the sum in
\xi_{1}
for general
n
can be divided into a double sum

\frac{1}{[\xi[n]:\xi[i]]}\sum\limits_{\xi_{1}\in\xi[n]/\sim_{i}}=\frac{1}{[\xi[n]:\xi[n_{0}]]}\sum\limits_{\xi_{0}\in\xi[n]/\sim_{n_{0}}}\frac{1}{[\xi[n_{0}]:\xi[i]]}\sum\limits_{\xi_{1}\in\xi_{0}[n_{0}]/\sim_{i}}

and the vanishing result for
n_{0}
can be applied to get the vanishing result for larger families.
As the proof inevitably requires case by case checking, we collect here in a table all necessary information combining parameterization of supercuspidal representations with (
6.87
) (
6.88
).

Case

c(\sigma)

c_{0}

c(\xi)

c(\xi^{\prime})

range of
k

p=2,d=0

2j+2

2j
